Option A is wrong because the meeting was not rescheduled. Option B is wrong because they never fixed the printer themselves.     Wesley: A permit system would definitely make this easier to support.

    The correct answer is A (Adding a resident permit system so locals park for free): Xiomara suggests 'they could add a resident permit system so locals park free and only visitors pay.' Option B is wrong because Xiomara supports metering, not removing the proposal. Option C is wrong because meters on every street is not proposed. Option D is wrong because a two hour limit is not mentioned.

Practice Test 9 Answer Key

  1. Listening Script

    I ordered a birthday cake for my son's party, but when I picked it up, the bakery had written the wrong name on it entirely, a completely different kid's name. The party was starting in two hours, so there wasn't time to have a new one made from scratch. The bakery manager offered to quickly scrape off the icing lettering and pipe on the correct name right there while I waited, which took about fifteen minutes. It wasn't perfect, you could still see a faint outline underneath, but it worked out fine for the party.

    The correct answer is D (They quickly re-piped the correct name over the old icing): the speaker says the manager 'offered to quickly scrape off the icing lettering and pipe on the correct name right there while I waited.' Option A is wrong because no refund is mentioned. Option C is wrong because a future free cake is not offered. Option B is wrong because there wasn't time to bake a brand new cake, so they fixed the existing one instead.
  2. Listening Script

    My internet went down the night before I had an important video interview for a new job. I called my provider, but they said a technician couldn't come until the following week. Panicking, I remembered my phone has a mobile hotspot feature, so I tested it and found the connection was actually pretty stable. I ended up doing the entire interview using my phone's hotspot instead of my home internet, and thankfully the call never dropped or lagged during the whole thing.

    The correct answer is C (They used their phone's mobile hotspot instead of home internet): the speaker says 'I ended up doing the entire interview using my phone's hotspot instead of my home internet.' Option A is wrong because they did not reschedule. Option B is wrong because a neighbour's internet is not mentioned. Option D is wrong because a coffee shop is not mentioned.
  3. Listening Script

    I planted tomatoes in my backyard garden this spring, but by midsummer the leaves were turning yellow and curling at the edges. I asked a coworker who gardens a lot, and she suggested it might be overwatering rather than a pest problem, since the soil looked constantly damp when I checked. I cut back on watering to just twice a week instead of daily, and within about two weeks the plants noticeably improved, with new green growth appearing at the top.

    The correct answer is B (They reduced watering from daily to twice a week): the speaker says 'I cut back on watering to just twice a week instead of daily.' Option A is wrong because a pesticide is not mentioned, the coworker suspected overwatering, not pests. Option C is wrong because replanting elsewhere is not mentioned. Option D is wrong because fertilizer is not discussed.
  4. Listening Script

    I showed up at the airport only to realize I had left my driver's licence at home, and it was my only piece of government identification for the flight. With boarding starting in under an hour, I called my roommate, who was thankfully still home, and asked her to photograph the licence and bring up my passport instead, which was also in my drawer. She rushed it over to the airport in a taxi, and I made the flight with about ten minutes to spare.

    The correct answer is A (She brought the speaker's passport to the airport by taxi): the speaker says the roommate 'rushed it over to the airport in a taxi,' referring to the passport she was asked to bring. Option B is wrong because emailing a photo to the airline is not what solved it, she brought actual ID. Option C is wrong because calling the airport is not mentioned. Option D is wrong because mailing is far too slow for the situation and not mentioned.
  5. Listening Script

    My washing machine started leaking water onto the laundry room floor every time I ran a cycle.     The correct answer is C (They ordered a replacement door seal and installed it themselves): the speaker says 'I ordered the replacement part... installing it myself took about twenty minutes with a screwdriver.' Option A is wrong because they avoided calling a technician. Option B is wrong because they repaired rather than replaced the machine. Option D is wrong because using towels as a workaround is not mentioned.

Option A is wrong because a refund and cancellation are not mentioned. Option C is wrong because waiting for the original car is not what happened. Option B is wrong because a different location is not mentioned.
  7. Listening Script

    Hi, it's Priya. Option B is wrong because she never contacts the landlord about the schedule. Option C is wrong because she is still preparing for her parents' visit, not cancelling it. Option D is wrong because bottled water is never mentioned; the problem is about laundry, not drinking water.
  8. Listening Script

Option A is wrong because the appointment happened as planned. Option B is wrong because the speaker considered a tow truck but decided against it. Option D is wrong because no taxi is mentioned; the ride came from a neighbour, not a taxi service.
  9. Listening Script

    Sam: Hey, are we still on for hiking at Lynn Canyon this Saturday?

    Jess: Works for me. I'll bring the sandwiches this time since you brought them last week.

    Sam: Deal. See you at one on Saturday.

Option B is wrong because they reschedule the time rather than cancel. Option C is wrong because no alternate trail is discussed, only the timing changes. Option D is wrong because Jess says she will bring the sandwiches this time, not Sam.

Option A is wrong because he praises the croissants as 'amazing.' Option B is wrong because he stayed and bought the croissants rather than leaving empty-handed. Option C is wrong because he plans to return, just earlier, not stop going to the bakery.

I'll pick some up this weekend.' Option A is wrong because Ahmed finds registry items like the stroller too impersonal. Option B is wrong for the same reason regarding baby clothes.     The correct answer is B (Downtown parking became too expensive for his daily commute): Carlos states, 'parking downtown got way too expensive, almost twenty dollars a day now,' as his reason for switching. Option A is wrong because no car trouble is mentioned. Option C is wrong because Carlos actually says the bus ride is longer, about forty minutes, not faster. Option D is wrong because he uses the extra time to read, not to exercise.
  14. Listening Script

  20. Listening Script

Option A is wrong because the splash pads are free, not fee-based. Option C is wrong because the heat wave is mentioned only as background context, not as the main topic. Option D is wrong because the report describes funding for a new project, not budget cuts.
  21. Listening Script

Option A is wrong because phasing out bus routes for bike lanes is never mentioned. Option B is wrong because raising money for road construction is not stated as a goal.   22. Listening Script

Option A is wrong because walk-ins are accepted throughout the clinic season, not only in the final week. Option B is wrong because the report never states that walk-ins must call ahead first.   23. Listening Script

Option D is wrong because planning meetings are not described as the weekend activity.
  24. Listening Script

    The city of Hamilton is testing a new streetlight system this fall designed to save energy by automatically dimming when no pedestrians or vehicles are nearby.     The correct answer is C (They automatically dim when no one is nearby and brighten with motion): The report explains the lights are 'designed to save energy by automatically dimming when no pedestrians or vehicles are nearby,' then brighten 'within seconds when someone approaches.' Option A is wrong because the lights dim in response to motion rather than switch off on a fixed overnight schedule. Option B is wrong because solar power is never mentioned. Option D is wrong because the dimming responds to motion sensors, not a fixed timer.
  25. Listening Script

    The correct answer is B (That employees might cram five days of work into only four): Ravi says, 'People might just end up cramming five days of work into four,' identifying this as his main concern. Option A is wrong because pay is never discussed in the conversation. Option C is wrong because client reactions are not mentioned. Option D is wrong because hiring new staff is not part of the discussion.
  26. Listening Script

    Elena: Have you seen the proposal to add a bike lane on Fourth Avenue?

    Jordan: Yeah, I have mixed feelings. Cyclists definitely need safer routes, but that street already gets pretty congested with cars.

    Elena: True, but studies from other cities show bike lanes can actually ease congestion over time by getting more people out of their cars.

    Jordan: Maybe, but I still think the city should survey local businesses first, since some worry about losing parking spots out front.

    Elena: That's fair. A survey couldn't hurt before anything gets built.

    The correct answer is D (Survey local businesses about their concerns first): Jordan states, 'I still think the city should survey local businesses first, since some worry about losing parking spots.' Option A is wrong because Jordan does not call for cancelling the project outright. Option B is wrong because building parking garages is never suggested. Option C is wrong because Jordan's suggestion is a survey of local businesses, not waiting on data from other cities.
  27. Listening Script

    Ben: Our apartment building's management wants to install security cameras in the hallways.

    Tara: I get the safety appeal, but doesn't that feel a bit invasive for a residential building?

    Ben: I was thinking the same thing at first, but honestly, after those package thefts last month, a lot of neighbours are asking for it.

    Tara: That's a fair point. I guess as long as the cameras only cover common areas and not right outside individual doors, I could live with it.

    Ben: Right, that's the plan they mentioned, common hallways and the lobby only.

    The correct answer is B (That the cameras only cover common areas, not individual doors): Tara says she could accept the cameras 'as long as the cameras only cover common areas and not right outside individual doors.' Option A is wrong because nighttime-only recording is never discussed. Option C is wrong because a resident vote is not mentioned as a condition. Option D is wrong because rent reduction is not part of the conversation.
  28. Listening Script

    Sophie: The school board is debating whether to start classes an hour later for teenagers.

    Daniel: I actually support that. Research keeps showing teens need more sleep and do better academically with a later start.

    Sophie: I hear that, but what about parents who need to drop kids off before work? A later start could really mess up family schedules.

    Daniel: That's true, though the board mentioned they might offer early supervised drop-off for kids who arrive before the new start time.

    Sophie: If that's part of the plan, then my worry is a lot smaller.

    The correct answer is B (That it could disrupt parents' schedules for dropping kids off): Sophie raises the concern, 'what about parents who need to drop kids off before work? A later start could really mess up family schedules.' Option A is wrong because Sophie does not dispute that teens need more sleep. Option C is wrong because reduced subjects are never mentioned. Option D is wrong because teachers' hours are not discussed in the conversation.
  29. Listening Script

    Kevin: Have you noticed how many food delivery apps have popped up in the last couple of years?

    Amara: Definitely. It's convenient, but I worry small restaurants are losing money to the commission fees those apps charge.

    Kevin: That's a real issue. Some places charge up to thirty percent per order, which barely leaves the restaurant with a profit.

    Amara: Right, and yet customers love the convenience so much that restaurants feel like they can't say no to joining.

    Kevin: It's a tough spot. Maybe more people ordering directly through a restaurant's own website would help.

    The correct answer is B (That high commission fees leave small restaurants with little profit): Amara says she worries 'small restaurants are losing money to the commission fees those apps charge,' and Kevin adds that fees can reach 'up to thirty percent per order.' Option A is wrong because ease of use is not discussed. Option C is wrong because driver pay is never mentioned. Option D is wrong because delivery speed is not part of the conversation.
  30. Listening Script

    Naomi: I saw the city is considering charging for parking at all the beach lots this summer.

    Liam: I actually don't mind that, since the money is supposed to go toward beach cleanup and lifeguard staffing.

    Liam: That's a good point. Maybe they need stricter enforcement on those side streets too, not just the paid lots.

    Naomi: Exactly, otherwise the residents living nearby end up dealing with the headache.

    The correct answer is C (That people might park illegally on nearby residential streets): Naomi says, 'I worry it'll push people to park illegally on nearby residential streets instead.' Option A is wrong because funding levels for lifeguards are not questioned. Option B is wrong because Naomi's concern is about paid parking, not free parking causing crowding. Option D is wrong because tourism levels are never mentioned.
  31. Listening Script

    The correct answer is D (That tree roots could damage sidewalks and driveways): Owen explains, 'a few homeowners are worried about tree roots damaging the sidewalks and their driveways over time.' Option A is wrong because blocked sunlight is never mentioned. Option B is wrong because insects are not discussed in the conversation. Option C is wrong because cost of maintenance is not raised as a concern.
  32. Listening Script

    Grace: I read that our workplace might switch to a fully open floor plan next year.

    Hassan: That could work for some teams, but for people doing detailed report writing like us, it might just mean more distractions.

    Grace: True. Maybe they'll at least keep a few quiet rooms available for focused work.

    The correct answer is D (That it would increase distractions for detailed, focused work): Hassan says an open plan 'for people doing detailed report writing like us, it might just mean more distractions.' Option A is wrong because cost is not discussed. Option B is wrong because desk space is never mentioned. Option C is wrong because the stated purpose of the change is to improve collaboration, not reduce it, and Hassan does not dispute that goal.
  33. Listening Script

    Reporter: We asked residents what they think about the city adding paid parking meters downtown.

    Speaker 1, Diane: Honestly, I think it's overdue. Free parking meant spots were always taken by people who worked downtown all day, leaving nothing for shoppers.

    Speaker 2, Marcus: I disagree completely. Small business owners like me are worried customers will just avoid downtown altogether and shop at the mall instead, where parking is free.

    Speaker 3, Rita: I'm somewhere in the middle. I think a small fee is fine, but the first thirty minutes should stay free for quick errands.

    The correct answer is C (Marcus): Marcus says he worries 'customers will just avoid downtown altogether and shop at the mall instead, where parking is free.' Option A is wrong because Diane actually supports paid parking, saying it is 'overdue.' Option B is wrong because Rita proposes a compromise involving a free first thirty minutes, not a warning about lost customers. Option D is wrong because the reporter only asks the question and does not give a personal opinion.
  34. Listening Script

    Reporter: What do you think about the city requiring all new homes to include solar panels?

    Speaker 1, Tom: I fully support it. Yes, it raises the upfront cost of a new home, but the long-term energy savings make it worthwhile for families.

    Speaker 2, Yuki: I understand the environmental goal, but for many first-time buyers, that extra upfront cost could price them out of the market entirely.

    Speaker 3, Carlos: I think the city should offer rebates to offset the cost instead of just making it mandatory with no support.

    The correct answer is C (Yuki): Yuki says the extra cost 'could price them out of the market entirely,' referring specifically to first-time buyers. Option A is wrong because Tom supports the rule and focuses on long-term savings, not affordability concerns. Option B is wrong because Carlos proposes rebates as a solution rather than focusing on first-time buyers being priced out. Option D is wrong because affordability is directly discussed by Yuki.
  35. Listening Script

    Reporter: We're asking people what they think about extending library hours on weekends.

    Speaker 3, Grace: I think it's a great idea overall, especially for working parents who can only bring their kids on Saturdays.

    The correct answer is B (Felix): Felix questions 'if the library even has the staff and budget to stay open longer without cutting other programs.' Option A is wrong because Wendy focuses on wanting quiet study time, not budget concerns. Option C is wrong because Grace discusses working parents, not funding. Option D is wrong because the reporter simply poses the question without offering a viewpoint.
  36. Listening Script

    Speaker 3, Chen: I agree with the environmental reasoning, but I think the city should give businesses a longer transition period, maybe two or three years.

    The correct answer is A (Bruno): Bruno says the switch 'would be expensive for small companies that would need to replace all their equipment at once.' Option B is wrong because Anita's focus is on noise, not business costs. Option C is wrong because Chen proposes a longer transition period rather than raising the cost issue directly. Option D is wrong because the equipment cost concern is raised specifically by Bruno, not equally by all three.
  37. Listening Script

    Reporter: We asked people about the proposal to build a new dog park near the community centre.

Option A is wrong because Leo supports the park and focuses on the lack of off-leash space. Option B is wrong because Jamal's concern is about the location's distance from the playground, not noise. Option D is wrong because the reporter only introduces the topic and expresses no opinion.
  38. Listening Script

    Reporter: What's your opinion on the new rule requiring helmets for all e-scooter riders downtown?

    Speaker 2, Omar: I get the safety concern, but I doubt many casual riders will actually carry a helmet with them everywhere they go.

    Speaker 3, Ivy: I agree with Omar that enforcement will be tricky, but maybe the scooter companies could offer helmets right at the rental stations.

    The correct answer is C (Ivy): Ivy suggests, 'maybe the scooter companies could offer helmets right at the rental stations.' Option A is wrong because Petra focuses on supporting the rule as a safety measure, without proposing this solution. Option B is wrong because Omar raises doubt about enforcement but does not suggest the rental-station idea. Option D is wrong because the rental-station suggestion comes only from Ivy, not from Petra or Omar.

Practice Test 10 Answer Key

  1. Listening Script

    So I booked a hotel in Victoria for the long weekend, but when I checked my email this morning, I noticed the confirmation showed the wrong dates, a full week off from what I actually wanted. I called the front desk right away, and luckily they still had a room available for my real dates, so they fixed the booking over the phone with no extra charge.

    The correct answer is D (The hotel fixed the booking over the phone at no extra cost): The speaker says the hotel 'fixed the booking over the phone with no extra charge.' Option A is wrong because the trip was not cancelled; the dates were corrected. Option B is wrong because the speaker kept the same hotel, not a different one. Option C is wrong because the message clearly states there was no extra charge for the correction.
  2. Listening Script

    I ordered a couch online and when it arrived, the fabric colour was completely different from what I saw on the website, more of a dark grey instead of the light beige I picked. I thought about just keeping it since returns can be a hassle, but I decided the colour really didn't match my living room, so I contacted the company and arranged a free pickup and exchange for the correct colour.

    The correct answer is D (Arrange a free pickup and exchange it for the correct colour): The speaker says, 'I contacted the company and arranged a free pickup and exchange for the correct colour.' Option A is wrong because the speaker only considered keeping it before deciding against that option. Option B is wrong because there is no mention of selling the couch or shopping elsewhere. Option C is wrong because dyeing the couch is never mentioned as a solution.
  3. Listening Script

    My daughter's school called saying she forgot her lunch at home, and I was already stuck in a meeting downtown with no way to leave. I couldn't drop it off myself, so I used a delivery app to send a sandwich and some fruit straight from a nearby grocery store to the school office. The staff said it arrived within twenty minutes, right before her lunch period started.

    The correct answer is A (The speaker used a delivery app to send food to the school): The speaker explains, 'I used a delivery app to send a sandwich and some fruit straight from a nearby grocery store to the school office.' Option B is wrong because the speaker could not leave the meeting. Option C is wrong because no neighbour is mentioned as helping. Option D is wrong because the cafeteria providing a free lunch is never mentioned; the solution came through a delivery app.
  4. Listening Script

    The heating in our apartment stopped working completely on one of the coldest nights this winter. I called the building superintendent, but he wasn't answering his phone that late, so my partner and I ended up plugging in two space heaters we borrowed from a neighbour just to get through the night. The superintendent came by first thing the next morning and fixed the furnace within an hour.

    The correct answer is A (They borrowed space heaters from a neighbour for the night): The speaker says they 'ended up plugging in two space heaters we borrowed from a neighbour just to get through the night.' Option B is wrong because staying at a hotel is never mentioned. Option C is wrong because they stayed in their own apartment with space heaters, not at a neighbour's place. Option D is wrong because the superintendent, not the speaker, fixed the furnace the next morning.
  5. Listening Script

    I was supposed to give a presentation at work this morning, but my laptop wouldn't turn on at all right before I left the house. I didn't have time to troubleshoot it, so I quickly emailed the slides to myself and used the office computer in the meeting room instead. It actually worked out fine, and nobody in the meeting even noticed the change.

    The correct answer is C (Emailed the slides and used the office computer instead): The speaker says, 'I quickly emailed the slides to myself and used the office computer in the meeting room instead.' Option A is wrong because the presentation still happened as scheduled. Option B is wrong because a coworker's personal laptop is never mentioned. Option D is wrong because the speaker used slides on the office computer, not memory alone.
  6. Listening Script

    My flight got delayed by four hours right as I was heading to the airport for a family reunion in Ottawa. Instead of just sitting around the gate the whole time, I called my cousin who lives near the airport, and she picked me up so we could grab an early dinner together before I had to head back for the new boarding time.

    The correct answer is D (Had an early dinner with a cousin who lives nearby): The speaker explains, 'she picked me up so we could grab an early dinner together before I had to head back for the new boarding time.' Option A is wrong because the speaker left the gate rather than waiting there. Option B is wrong because there is no mention of booking a different flight. Option C is wrong because a hotel room is never mentioned.
  7. Listening Script

    The venue we booked for my brother's birthday party cancelled on us just two days before the event, saying there was a plumbing issue in the building. Everyone actually said they liked it better than the original venue.

    The correct answer is C (The party moved to a friend's backyard with rented decorations): The speaker says, 'a friend offered her backyard, so we quickly rented some folding tables and string lights and turned it into an outdoor party instead.' Option A is wrong because the original venue was never repaired; the party moved elsewhere. Option B is wrong because the party still happened rather than being rescheduled. Option D is wrong because a replacement party clearly did take place.
  8. Listening Script

    My internet went down the night before I had an important video call with a client, and the provider's earliest technician appointment was two days away. I ended up driving to a coffee shop with free wifi near my house and taking the call from there instead.     The correct answer is C (Took the call from a coffee shop with free wifi): The speaker explains, 'I ended up driving to a coffee shop with free wifi near my house and taking the call from there instead.' Option A is wrong because the call happened on schedule, not after the repair visit. Option B is wrong because a neighbour's wifi is never mentioned. Option D is wrong because the call still took place; it was not cancelled.
  9. Listening Script

    Fatima: Are we still meeting for brunch on Sunday before your parents arrive?

Option A is wrong because the diner's hours are never mentioned as an issue. Option B is wrong because Fatima does not mention a conflict; James is the one requesting the change. Option D is wrong because the sister is only added to the plan afterward, not the reason for the date change.
  10. Listening Script

Option D is wrong because visitors are not discussed as a cause of the delay.

Option A is wrong because no friend is mentioned as convincing him. Option B is wrong because the class cost is never discussed.   13. Listening Script

Option A is wrong because sale prices are never mentioned. Option C is wrong because Paul made the decision on his own, before Renata brings up her own experience. Option D is wrong because free time is not discussed as a reason.
  14. Listening Script

    This is an automated message from the Sunridge Recreation Centre. Our indoor pool will be closed for routine maintenance from Monday to Wednesday next week.     The correct answer is A (They will be moved to the outdoor pool, weather permitting): The message states, 'All swimming lessons scheduled during that time will be moved to the outdoor pool instead, weather permitting.' Option B is wrong because the lessons continue, just at a different location, not cancelled for part of the week. Option C is wrong because no other recreation centre is mentioned. Option D is wrong because the lessons are moved during the closure, not paused until it ends.
  15. Listening Script

  20. Listening Script

  22. Listening Script

  23. Listening Script

  24. Listening Script

  25. Listening Script

  26. Listening Script

Option D is wrong because closing the rink for the season is never suggested.
  27. Listening Script

    Priya: The homeowners association wants to change our garbage pickup from weekly to every other week.

    Marco: I get that it might save money, but with a big family like mine, our bins would overflow within a few days.

    Priya: That's a valid worry. Maybe they could keep weekly pickup for garbage but move recycling and compost to every other week instead.

    Marco: That actually makes more sense. Garbage tends to smell faster than the other bins anyway.

    Priya: Exactly, I'll suggest that at the next meeting.

    The correct answer is B (Keeping garbage weekly, but moving recycling and compost every other week): Priya suggests, 'they could keep weekly pickup for garbage but move recycling and compost to every other week instead.' Option A is wrong because monthly pickup is never proposed. Option C is wrong because an extra fee for large families is not part of the discussion. Option D is wrong because ending garbage collection in most neighbourhoods is not suggested; only the frequency is adjusted.
  28. Listening Script

    Wei: I noticed the office cafeteria might stop offering free coffee to save on costs.

    Brianna: That would be a shame, a lot of people rely on that in the morning.

    Wei: Agreed, though management says the machines and beans cost more than expected this year.

    Brianna: Maybe instead of cutting it completely, they could just charge a small fee, like fifty cents a cup, to cover part of the cost.

    Wei: That sounds fair. I'd rather pay a little than lose it altogether.

    The correct answer is A (Charging a small fee, such as fifty cents per cup): Brianna proposes, 'they could just charge a small fee, like fifty cents a cup, to cover part of the cost.' Option B is wrong because replacing the machines is never suggested. Option C is wrong because reduced hours are not part of the discussion. Option D is wrong because bringing coffee from home is not mentioned as an alternative.
  29. Listening Script

    Hassan: The city council is debating whether to add more speed bumps on residential streets.

    Lena: I'm generally in favour, our street gets a lot of speeding, especially near the school.

    Hassan: I agree it's a problem, but speed bumps can also slow down ambulances and fire trucks responding to emergencies.

    Lena: That's true, I hadn't thought about that. Maybe speed cameras would be a better option instead, since they slow drivers down without blocking emergency vehicles.

    Hassan: That actually makes a lot of sense to me.

    The correct answer is D (Installing speed cameras instead of speed bumps): After Hassan raises the emergency vehicle concern, Lena says, 'Maybe speed cameras would be a better option instead, since they slow drivers down without blocking emergency vehicles.' Option A is wrong because Lena still wants some form of traffic calming, not none at all. Option B is wrong because hiring more police officers is never mentioned. Option C is wrong because lowering the speed limit alone is not her suggestion; she proposes cameras specifically.
  30. Listening Script

    Tamara: Our neighbourhood watch group wants to install motion-activated lights along the back alley.

    Roy: I think that's a great idea, that alley has been really dark and a bit sketchy at night.

    Tamara: Agreed, but a few residents are worried the lights might disturb people sleeping in bedrooms that face the alley.

    Roy: Good point. Maybe they could choose lights with a shorter timer, so they turn off again quickly after motion stops.

    Tamara: That seems like a reasonable way to balance safety and everyone's sleep.

    The correct answer is B (Using lights with a shorter timer that turn off quickly): Roy suggests, 'they could choose lights with a shorter timer, so they turn off again quickly after motion stops.' Option A is wrong because Roy supports the project rather than pausing it. Option C is wrong because weekend-only lighting is never mentioned. Option D is wrong because relocating the lights away from windows is not his proposed solution; adjusting the timer is.
  31. Listening Script

    Priya: I think our office should switch to the four day work week they're testing at other companies.

    Tom: I don't know, Priya. Four ten hour days sounds exhausting to me.

    Priya: Maybe at first, but you'd get a full extra day off every week for errands or family time.

    Tom: True, but our clients call us on Fridays all the time. Who would answer the phones?

    Priya: We could split the team so half work Monday to Thursday and half work Tuesday to Friday.

    Tom: That could actually work. I'd want to see it tried for a month before we commit to it fully.

    The correct answer is C (He worries clients calling on Fridays would have nobody to answer them): Tom directly asks, 'who would answer the phones?' after noting that clients call the office all the time on Fridays. Option B describes his first, milder comment about tiredness, but that is not the concern he presses on. Option D is not mentioned anywhere in the conversation. Option A, about pay, is never raised by either speaker.
  32. Listening Script

    Aiden: Have you noticed the new bike lanes going in along Bridge Street?

    Sana: I have. I think it's a great idea. Cyclists were always squeezed onto the sidewalk before.

    Aiden: I agree cyclists need somewhere safe, but the city removed a whole lane of parking to do it.

    Sana: Businesses were worried about that, but the city says a bus stop and a loading zone are still there.

    Aiden: I guess I'm mostly concerned about deliveries. Trucks already double park on that street.

    Sana: That's fair. Maybe they'll add a dedicated loading window later in the morning.

    The correct answer is D (He is concerned that delivery trucks will have nowhere convenient to stop): Aiden says he is 'mostly concerned about deliveries' and notes that trucks already double park on the street, implying it will get worse with less parking space. Option C is wrong because Aiden agrees cyclists need somewhere safe. Option A is incorrect since Sana confirms the bus stop is still there. Option B is false because Aiden never opposes the bike lanes themselves, only the parking loss.
  33. Listening Script

    The city asked residents what they think about extending library hours on weekends. Here is what some people said.

    Marcus: I use the library for quiet study on Sunday afternoons, so later hours would help me a lot.

    Devon: My kids go to storytime on Saturday mornings already. I don't think we need Sunday hours too.

    Renata: I work retail on weekends, so I can only ever visit the library on weekday evenings anyway.

    The correct answer is B (Marcus, because he uses the library for quiet study on Sunday afternoons): Marcus states directly that he uses the library on Sunday afternoons and that later hours 'would help me a lot.' Devon, in option C, says he does not think Sunday hours are needed since his kids already have a Saturday program. Renata, in option D, explains she can only go on weekday evenings, so Sunday hours would not affect her. Option A is false since two of the three speakers do mention weekend library use.
  34. Listening Script

    A radio host asked callers whether the city should build a new dog park downtown. Here are three responses.

    Wendy: My condo building doesn't allow a yard, so a nearby dog park would be wonderful for my dog.

    Colin: I don't own a dog, but I think the land would be better used for a community garden instead.

    Priti: I love the idea, but I worry the noise from a dog park could bother the seniors' residence next door.

    The correct answer is B (Priti, because she worries the noise could disturb the nearby seniors' residence): Priti says she loves the idea but worries the noise 'could bother the seniors' residence next door,' which is a concern tied directly to the location. Wendy, in option D, explains why she personally wants the park but raises no location concern. Colin, in option A, proposes a different use for the land rather than objecting to its location. Option C is incorrect because Priti clearly raises a location-related worry.
  35. Listening Script

    A community newsletter asked three neighbours what they think of the proposed streetlight upgrade. Here is what they said.

    Hassan: The current lights are so dim that I feel unsafe walking my dog after dinner in the winter.

    Lucia: Brighter LED lights sound fine, but I hope the city chooses a warm colour instead of a harsh blue-white glare.

    Owen: Honestly, I think the money would be better spent fixing the potholes on our street first.

    The correct answer is B (Lucia, because she hopes the city picks a warm light colour rather than a harsh glare): Lucia says the brighter lights 'sound fine' but she hopes for a warm colour, showing support with a specific preference. Hassan, in option A, supports the upgrade for safety reasons but states no preference about how it is done. Owen, in option C, opposes the upgrade in favour of pothole repairs. Option D is false because Hassan and Lucia both support the upgrade.
  36. Listening Script

    A local podcast asked residents for their opinion on the new farmers market held every Thursday evening. Here is what they said.

    Grace: I love that I can pick up fresh vegetables on my way home from work without a special trip.

    Tariq: The prices are honestly higher than the grocery store, so I only go there once in a while.

    Beatrice: I mostly go for the live music. The food is nice, but the atmosphere is what keeps me coming back.

    The correct answer is C (He finds the prices at the market are higher than at the grocery store): Tariq says the prices are 'honestly higher than the grocery store,' which is why he only goes 'once in a while.' Option D describes Beatrice's reason for attending, not Tariq's. Option A is not mentioned by any speaker. Option B is never stated; Tariq gives price as his reason, not his schedule.
  37. Listening Script

    A survey asked commuters what they think about the city's new express bus route downtown. Here are three answers.

    Noah: The express bus cut my commute time nearly in half, which has made mornings so much less stressful.

    Chidi: It's fast, but it only stops every twenty minutes, so I still have to plan my whole morning around it.

    Ines: I haven't tried it yet because the closest stop is still a fifteen minute walk from my apartment.

    The correct answer is A (He says the bus is fast, but its infrequent stops require careful planning): Chidi says the bus 'is fast' but 'only stops every twenty minutes,' so he still has to plan his morning around it. Option B is Ines's comment about the walk to the nearest stop, not Chidi's. Option C describes Noah's experience of a much shorter commute. Option D describes Ines, who says she has not tried the route yet.
  38. Listening Script

    A local news segment asked residents what they think about the city adding recycling bins to every park. Here are their comments.

    Farrah: It's about time. I always end up carrying my empty water bottle home because there's nowhere to recycle it.

    Desmond: I support it, though I hope the city also commits to emptying the bins regularly so they don't overflow.

    Jules: I think it's a nice idea, but I doubt many people will bother to sort their recycling properly in a park.

    The correct answer is A (Jules, because he doubts many park visitors will sort their recycling properly): Jules says he 'doubts many people will bother to sort their recycling properly in a park,' which is a clear statement of doubt about use. Farrah, in option C, explains a problem the bins would solve, not a doubt about them. Desmond, in option D, supports the idea but raises a maintenance hope, not doubt about usage. Option B is incorrect since Jules clearly expresses that doubt.

Practice Test 11 Answer Key

  1. Listening Script

    Denise's dishwasher stopped draining properly last week, leaving standing water at the bottom after every cycle. She checked the filter first and cleaned out some food debris, but the problem continued. She then looked up the model online and found a video showing that the drain hose often kinks behind the machine. Denise pulled the dishwasher out slightly, straightened the hose, and ran a test cycle. The water drained completely afterward, so she decided not to call a repair technician after all.

    The correct answer is C (She straightened a kinked hose behind the machine herself): the passage says Denise 'pulled the dishwasher out slightly, straightened the hose, and ran a test cycle,' after which the water drained completely. Option B is wrong because she decided not to call a technician. Option D is never mentioned; she kept her existing dishwasher. Option A describes her first attempt, cleaning the filter, which did not solve the problem.
  2. Listening Script

    Marcus booked a hotel room for a work trip to Winnipeg, but when he arrived the front desk told him the room had been given to someone else due to a system error. The hotel was fully booked, so no other room was available there. The front desk clerk apologized and offered to call a sister property two blocks away, cover the price difference, and arrange a taxi. Marcus agreed, and within twenty minutes he was checked into the other hotel at no extra cost to him.

    The correct answer is D (The clerk arranged a room at a sister hotel and covered the cost difference): the passage states the clerk 'offered to call a sister property two blocks away, cover the price difference, and arrange a taxi,' and Marcus was checked in 'at no extra cost.' Option B is not mentioned; Marcus stayed on his trip. Option C is incorrect since the hotel covered the price difference, not Marcus. Option A is false because he was checked in within twenty minutes, not after hours of waiting.
  3. Listening Script

    Yuki noticed her monthly phone bill had suddenly doubled, even though she hadn't changed her plan. She called her provider and was told a roaming charge from a trip she took last month had been added late. Yuki explained she had purchased a travel add-on before the trip specifically to avoid that charge. After checking her account history, the representative confirmed the add-on had been active and removed the incorrect roaming fee, reducing her bill back to its usual amount.

    The correct answer is C (A roaming charge was added even though she had a travel add-on active): the passage explains a roaming charge from her trip 'had been added late' despite Yuki having purchased a travel add-on to avoid it, which the representative later confirmed. Option B is not mentioned in the passage at all. Option D is the opposite of what happened, since she did buy the add-on. Option A is not stated; the issue was a roaming fee, not a duplicate plan charge.
  4. Listening Script

    Oliver's landlord informed him that the building's water would be shut off for repairs on the same day Oliver was hosting a small dinner party. Worried about how to cook and clean up without water, Oliver decided to move the dinner to a nearby park instead. He picked up food that didn't need cooking, borrowed a folding table from a neighbour, and brought bottled water for drinks. His guests ended up enjoying the picnic-style dinner just as much as the original plan.

    The correct answer is C (He moved the dinner to a nearby park and served food that needed no cooking): the passage states Oliver 'decided to move the dinner to a nearby park' and 'picked up food that didn't need cooking.' Option A is incorrect since the party stayed on the same day, just at a different location. Option B is never mentioned; Oliver did not ask the landlord to change the schedule. Option D is false because the dinner still happened and the guests enjoyed it.
  5. Listening Script

    Fatima's laptop kept freezing whenever she opened more than a few browser tabs at once, which made working from home difficult. She assumed she needed a new laptop, but a coworker suggested checking how much storage space was left first. Fatima discovered her hard drive was almost completely full of old photos and downloads. After deleting unnecessary files and moving her photos to an external drive, her laptop ran smoothly again without any hardware changes at all.

    The correct answer is A (The laptop's hard drive was almost completely full of files): the passage says Fatima 'discovered her hard drive was almost completely full of old photos and downloads,' and freeing up space fixed the freezing. Option B is not mentioned anywhere in the passage. Option C is a misreading; the coworker only suggested checking storage, not installing anything. Option D is never discussed, since the issue was about tabs freezing, not internet speed.
  6. Listening Script

    Greg ordered a couch online, but when it arrived, he realized it was the wrong colour, a dark grey instead of the beige he had chosen. He contacted the store's customer service, expecting a long wait, but the representative offered two choices: a full refund with free return pickup, or keeping the grey couch at a twenty percent discount since it was already assembled. Greg looked around his living room and decided the grey actually matched his rug better, so he kept it.

    The correct answer is D (He kept the grey couch after the store offered him a twenty percent discount): the passage explains Greg 'decided the grey actually matched his rug better, so he kept it' after being offered the discount option. Option B is incorrect because he never sent the couch back or reordered. Option C is not mentioned in the passage at all. Option A is wrong since Greg chose to keep the couch rather than take the refund.
  7. Listening Script

    Renata's flight home from a conference was cancelled due to a mechanical issue, and the airline's app showed the next available flight was not until two days later. Rather than wait, Renata called the airline directly and asked about flights from a nearby airport instead. The agent found a seat on a flight leaving that evening from an airport forty minutes away, and Renata took a shuttle there in time, arriving home only a few hours later than originally planned.

    The correct answer is C (She found a flight from a nearby airport by calling the airline directly): the passage states Renata 'called the airline directly and asked about flights from a nearby airport,' and the agent found her a seat that evening. Option D is not mentioned; she took a shuttle to another airport, not a road trip home. Option A is the opposite of what she did, since she avoided waiting two days. Option B is never stated in the passage.
  8. Listening Script

    Every time Sanjay's upstairs neighbour did laundry late at night, the washing machine's vibrations kept him awake. He considered complaining to the building manager right away, but decided to first leave a polite note explaining the issue. His neighbour responded apologetically, explaining they hadn't realized how loud it was, and agreed to run the machine only before ten at night going forward. The noise stopped completely, and Sanjay never needed to involve the manager.

    The correct answer is A (A polite note led his neighbour to agree to do laundry earlier at night): the passage says Sanjay left a note, and the neighbour 'agreed to run the machine only before ten at night going forward,' which stopped the noise. Option C is incorrect because the manager was never contacted. Option D is not mentioned anywhere in the passage. Option B is never stated as part of the resolution.
  9. Listening Script

    Ben: Are we still on for hiking this Saturday, or is the weather supposed to turn bad again?

    Claire: I checked this morning and it looks clear until about two in the afternoon, then rain moves in.

    Ben: Okay, so if we start early we should be fine. What time were you thinking?

    Claire: Maybe eight? That gives us a solid five hours before the rain shows up.

    Ben: Sounds good. I'll bring the trail snacks if you bring the water.

    Claire: Deal. I'll text you Friday night just in case the forecast changes again.

    The correct answer is D (Eight in the morning, to finish before the rain arrives in the afternoon): Claire suggests starting at 'maybe eight' because it gives them 'a solid five hours before the rain shows up' around two in the afternoon. Option A confuses the time rain is expected with the start time, which is the opposite of the plan. Option B misreads Friday night, which is only when Claire will text an update. Option C is not mentioned as a factor in choosing the start time.
  10. Listening Script

    Nadia: I finally tried that new bakery on Elm Street you told me about.

    Owen: Oh nice, what did you get?

    Nadia: I got a cinnamon roll, but honestly it was way too sweet for me, almost like eating pure sugar.

    Owen: Really? I always get their sourdough loaf instead. Have you tried that?

    Nadia: Not yet, but if you say it's good I'll grab one next weekend when I go back.

    Owen: Definitely worth it. Just go early, they usually sell out by noon.

    The correct answer is B (She thought it was far too sweet for her personal taste): Nadia says the cinnamon roll 'was way too sweet for me, almost like eating pure sugar.' Option A is not mentioned; there is no comment about it being undercooked or a refund. Option C is the opposite of what Nadia says about the roll. Option D is incorrect because Nadia did try the cinnamon roll, she just did not enjoy it.
  11. Listening Script

    Trevor: My sister's baby shower is next Sunday and I still haven't found a gift.

    Priya: What about something off her registry? That's usually the safest bet.

    Trevor: I checked, but almost everything on there is already bought except a really expensive stroller.

    Priya: You could always go in on the stroller with a couple of other family members.

    Trevor: That's actually a smart idea. I'll call my cousin tonight and see if she wants to split it.

    Priya: Let me know how it goes. I still need to pick something out for the shower too.

    The correct answer is B (He plans to call his cousin about splitting the cost of the stroller): Trevor says the idea to split the cost 'is actually a smart idea' and that he 'will call my cousin tonight and see if she wants to split it.' Option A is incorrect because the only item left on the registry is the expensive stroller, not a small item. Option C is not supported, since Trevor is actively planning to get a gift. Option D misattributes the plan, since Trevor is calling his cousin, not asking Priya.
  12. Listening Script

    Simone: Do you want to come over Friday night? I'm making that pasta dish you liked last time.

    Kwame: I'd love to, but I promised my nephew I'd take him to his hockey practice that evening.

    Simone: No problem, how about Saturday instead? I'm free most of the day.

    Kwame: Saturday works great for me. Should I bring anything, maybe dessert?

    Simone: That would be perfect, something simple is totally fine.

    Kwame: I'll pick up something from that bakery near my place then. See you Saturday.

    The correct answer is D (He promised to take his nephew to a hockey practice that evening): Kwame explains he 'promised my nephew I'd take him to his hockey practice that evening,' which is why Friday does not work. Option B is not mentioned as his reason for being unavailable. Option C describes something Kwame plans for Saturday, not a reason he is busy Friday. Option A is incorrect since the conversation gives a specific reason, not a general busy week.
  13. Listening Script

    Leo: I'm thinking about repainting the kitchen this weekend. What colour do you think?

    Maya: Honestly, I still love the pale yellow, but you mentioned wanting something calmer a while back.

    Leo: Right, I was leaning toward a soft sage green instead, something more relaxing in the mornings.

    Maya: That could look really nice with the wooden cabinets we already have.

    Leo: Good, then I'll pick up the sage green paint on my way home from work tomorrow.

    Maya: Perfect, I'll clear the counters Saturday morning so we can get an early start.

    The correct answer is A (A soft sage green, since it feels calmer in the mornings): Leo says he was 'leaning toward a soft sage green instead, something more relaxing in the mornings,' and then confirms he'll buy that paint tomorrow. Option D describes the current colour Maya mentions, not the new choice. Option B, bright white, is never mentioned in the conversation. Option C is incorrect because Leo has already made his decision by the end of the conversation.
  14. Listening Script

    Thank you for calling the Riverside Community Centre. Our fall registration for evening yoga classes opens this Monday at nine in the morning. Classes run Tuesday and Thursday evenings from six thirty to seven thirty, starting the first week of October. The cost is eighty five dollars for a ten week session, and space is limited to twenty participants per class. To register, please visit our front desk in person or call back during regular business hours.

    The correct answer is A (This Monday at nine in the morning): the message states 'our fall registration for evening yoga classes opens this Monday at nine in the morning.' Option B describes when the classes themselves start, not registration. Option C confuses the class day with the registration date. Option D is incorrect because registration has a specific opening time, not an immediate open window.
  15. Listening Script

    Attention passengers, the four fifteen train to Kitchener will now be departing from platform three instead of platform one due to a signal issue further down the line. Passengers already seated on platform one should proceed to platform three immediately using the overhead walkway. The train will still depart at its scheduled time of four fifteen, but boarding will close five minutes earlier than usual to allow for the platform change.

    The correct answer is A (The train will now depart from platform three instead of platform one): the announcement states the train 'will now be departing from platform three instead of platform one due to a signal issue.' Option D is incorrect because the departure time itself has not changed. Option B is not mentioned at all in the announcement. Option C is the opposite of what is stated, since boarding closes five minutes earlier, not later.
  16. Listening Script

    Welcome to the guided tour of the Heritage Museum. We'll begin in the main lobby, then move through the pioneer settlement exhibit, followed by the railway history hall. The full tour takes about ninety minutes, and photography without flash is permitted in every exhibit except the archives room.

    The correct answer is C (The third floor textile gallery is closed today for cleaning): the guide explains 'the third floor textile gallery is closed today for cleaning, so we will skip that stop.' Option B incorrectly names the railway hall as closed, when in fact the group spends extra time there instead. Option D is not mentioned anywhere in the passage. Option A is false, since photography without flash is allowed in every exhibit except the archives room.
  17. Listening Script

    Good afternoon everyone, before we start the fire drill I want to review the procedure. When the alarm sounds, leave your workstation immediately without gathering personal items, and exit through the nearest marked stairwell, not the elevators. Once outside, gather at the assembly point in the north parking lot, away from the building entrance. A floor warden will take attendance there, so please wait until your name is confirmed before leaving the area.

    The correct answer is D (Gather at the assembly point in the north parking lot for attendance): the instructions state employees should 'gather at the assembly point in the north parking lot' where 'a floor warden will take attendance there.' Option C contradicts the instruction to leave without gathering personal items. Option A is incorrect because the assembly point is described as away from the building entrance, and elevators are not to be used at all. Option B is the opposite of the instruction to wait until a name is confirmed.
  18. Listening Script

    You've reached the after hours line for Maple Grove Veterinary Clinic. Our regular hours are Monday to Friday, eight in the morning until six in the evening, and Saturday from nine until two. If this is a true emergency outside those hours, please call the Northside Animal Emergency Hospital at the number on our website. For non urgent matters, please leave a message after the tone and our staff will return your call the next business day.

    The correct answer is A (Call the Northside Animal Emergency Hospital using the number on the website): the message instructs that for a true emergency outside regular hours, callers should 'call the Northside Animal Emergency Hospital at the number on our website.' Option C describes what to do for non urgent matters, not emergencies. Option D is incorrect because an emergency would not wait until the next open hours. Option B is not supported, since walk ins for minor issues are not the same as handling a true emergency.
  19. Listening Script

    Reminder for all conference attendees: badge pickup is located in the east lobby and opens tomorrow at seven thirty in the morning, one hour before the first keynote session begins. Please bring a government issued photo ID along with your printed or digital confirmation email. Attendees registering for workshops will also need to select their session preferences at the badge pickup table, since workshop spots are assigned on a first come first served basis.

    The correct answer is B (A government issued photo ID and their confirmation email): the reminder states attendees should 'bring a government issued photo ID along with your printed or digital confirmation email.' Option A leaves out the required photo ID mentioned in the passage. Option C misreads the instruction, since workshop preferences are selected at the table, not written in advance. Option D, cash payment, is never mentioned anywhere in the reminder.
  20. Listening Script

    In local news, the city announced this week that construction on the new pedestrian bridge over Cedar Creek is now ahead of schedule, with an opening date moved up to early November instead of the originally planned January. The bridge will connect the riverside trail to the downtown core, cutting the walking commute for many residents by nearly fifteen minutes. City officials say favourable weather this summer allowed crews to work extra days without delay.

    The correct answer is B (Favourable weather this summer let crews work extra days without delay): the report states 'favourable weather this summer allowed crews to work extra days without delay,' which moved the opening earlier. Option A is not mentioned anywhere in the report. Option C, a resident vote, is never discussed. Option D is incorrect, since the report presents the change as genuine progress, not a planning error.
  21. Listening Script

    Health officials are reminding residents that the seasonal flu vaccine is now available at pharmacies and clinics across the province. This year's vaccine targets three strains expected to circulate this winter, and officials recommend that seniors, young children, and pregnant individuals get vaccinated as early as possible. Appointments are not required at most pharmacy locations, though officials note that wait times may be longer on weekends.

    The correct answer is D (Seniors, young children, and pregnant individuals): the report states officials 'recommend that seniors, young children, and pregnant individuals get vaccinated as early as possible.' Option C is not mentioned in the report at all. Option A, travel plans, is never discussed as a factor. Option B is not stated; the recommendation is based on the groups named, not appointment status.
  22. Listening Script

    The city's public works department is warning residents about a temporary water main flush beginning Monday in the west end neighbourhood. During the flush, residents may notice discoloured or cloudy tap water for a short time. Officials say the water remains safe to drink, but recommend running the cold tap for a few minutes before use if discolouration is noticed. The flush is expected to be completed by Wednesday evening.

    The correct answer is B (Run the cold tap for a few minutes before using the water): the report recommends residents 'run the cold tap for a few minutes before use if discolouration is noticed.' Option D is incorrect since officials say the water remains safe to drink throughout. Option A is not mentioned as a recommended action in the report. Option C, boiling water, is never suggested anywhere in the passage.
  23. Listening Script

    A new report released this week shows that use of the city's bike share program more than doubled over the past year, with the highest growth seen among commuters travelling to work downtown. Officials credit the increase to twelve new docking stations added last spring and a discounted monthly pass introduced for students. The city says it plans to add another eight stations in the suburbs by next summer to meet the growing demand.

    The correct answer is D (Add another eight docking stations in the suburbs): the report states 'the city says it plans to add another eight stations in the suburbs by next summer to meet the growing demand.' Option C is the opposite of what is described, since the discounted pass helped drive growth and is not being removed. Option A, doubling prices, is never mentioned. Option B is incorrect since the twelve stations added last spring are not being closed.
  24. Listening Script

    A local charity is reporting record turnout for its annual coat drive this year, collecting over three thousand winter coats for families in need, nearly a thousand more than last year's total. Organizers say a new partnership with several local schools helped boost donations, since students collected coats directly from classmates and neighbours. The coats will be distributed at community centres across the city starting next week, ahead of the coldest part of winter.

    The correct answer is A (A new partnership with several local schools that collected coats): the report explains 'a new partnership with several local schools helped boost donations, since students collected coats directly from classmates and neighbours.' Option B is not mentioned and is contradicted by the record turnout described. Option C, a television ad, is never mentioned in the report. Option D is not discussed; only the distribution timing and location for this year are mentioned, not a change from prior years.
  25. Listening Script

    Petra: Management wants to switch our team meetings from Monday mornings to Friday afternoons. What do you think?

    Dario: I actually like Monday mornings. It sets the direction for the whole week ahead of time.

    Petra: That's fair, but by Friday we'd already know what got done that week and what's left over.

    Dario: True, but people are usually mentally checked out by Friday afternoon, ready for the weekend.

    Petra: Maybe, but we could keep it short, just twenty minutes to wrap things up.

    Dario: A shorter meeting might change my mind. Let's suggest that compromise to management.

    The correct answer is C (He thinks people are usually too tired and distracted by Friday afternoon): Dario says 'people are usually mentally checked out by Friday afternoon, ready for the weekend.' Option D is not something Dario states; length is only discussed later as a compromise. Option A is never mentioned by either speaker. Option B is not supported by the conversation, which focuses on the meeting's timing, not management's role.
  26. Listening Script

    Wei: Did you see the proposal to add a fee for reserving study rooms at the library?

    Fiona: I did, and honestly I understand it. People book rooms and then never show up.

    Wei: Sure, but a fee might stop students who genuinely can't afford it from booking at all.

    Fiona: That's a good point. Maybe a small deposit that's refunded if you actually show up would work better.

    Wei: I like that. It discourages no shows without permanently charging anyone who follows through.

    Fiona: I'll bring that suggestion up at the next library board meeting.

    The correct answer is A (A refundable deposit that is returned if the person actually shows up): Fiona proposes 'a small deposit that's refunded if you actually show up,' which Wei then agrees would discourage no shows fairly. Option D, a booking ban, is not suggested by either speaker. Option B, a paid membership, is never mentioned in the conversation. Option C is incorrect since both speakers are trying to improve the reservation system, not remove it.
  27. Listening Script

    Hector: Our building's management wants to install security cameras in the hallways. I'm not thrilled about it.

    Ingrid: Really? I actually feel safer knowing there's some record if a package goes missing.

    Hector: I get that, but I worry about who has access to the footage and how long it's kept.

    Ingrid: That's fair. I'd want clear rules about that written down before they're installed.

    Hector: Exactly. If management agrees to that, I'd be a lot more comfortable with the idea.

    Ingrid: Let's write a letter together asking for those rules before the vote next month.

    The correct answer is A (He worries about who has access to the footage and how long it is kept): Hector states directly, 'I worry about who has access to the footage and how long it's kept.' Option D is Ingrid's supportive view about missing packages, not Hector's concern. Option B, cost, is never mentioned in the conversation. Option C is not stated; missing packages are only used as an example by Ingrid, not disputed by Hector.
  28. Listening Script

    Camille: The school board is considering starting classes thirty minutes later next year.

    Julian: I've read that teenagers naturally sleep later, so that change could really help students focus.

    Camille: I agree with the idea, but it would push school pickup later for parents with jobs to get to.

    Julian: That's true. Maybe they could offer an early drop off program for families who need it.

    Camille: That would help a lot. I think most parents would support the change with that option added.

    Julian: I'll mention that idea at the parent council meeting this week.

    The correct answer is B (She worries it would push school pickup later for working parents): Camille says the later start 'would push school pickup later for parents with jobs to get to.' Option A misrepresents Camille, who agrees with the sleep research Julian mentions. Option C is not discussed; the conversation focuses on solutions, not the board's decision. Option D contradicts Camille, who agrees that the change 'could really help students focus.'
  29. Listening Script

    Amos: I heard the grocery store on Fifth Avenue is switching entirely to self checkout.

    Beatriz: I'm not a fan. I like chatting with the cashiers, and it feels less personal now.

    Amos: I get that, but self checkout is usually faster when I'm just grabbing a few items.

    Beatriz: That part I agree with, especially in the evenings when lines get long.

    Amos: Maybe they'll keep at least one staffed lane open for people who prefer it.

    Beatriz: I hope so. Otherwise older customers who aren't comfortable with the machines will struggle.

    The correct answer is B (She enjoys chatting with cashiers and finds it feels more personal): Beatriz says she likes 'chatting with the cashiers, and it feels less personal now' without them, implying she values that human interaction. Option A is incorrect since Amos, not Beatriz, points out self checkout is often faster. Option C, cost to the store, is never mentioned in the conversation. Option D is not stated about Beatriz herself; she raises the concern about older customers instead.
  30. Listening Script

    Roland: The parks department wants to remove the old wooden playground and put in a rubber surfaced one.

    Selin: I think that's a good move. The wood has splinters and gets slippery when it rains.

    Roland: I agree it needs updating, but I hope they keep some of the classic equipment, like the tall slide.

    Selin: That's fair, kids do seem to love that slide specifically.

    Roland: If they redesign it with the same layout but safer materials, I think everyone would be happy.

    Selin: I'll suggest that at the next community meeting so residents can weigh in too.

    The correct answer is B (The tall slide, or at least a similar layout with safer materials): Roland says he hopes 'they keep some of the classic equipment, like the tall slide,' and later suggests keeping 'the same layout but safer materials.' Option A is incorrect since Roland agrees the wooden surface needs updating due to splinters and slipperiness. Option C misstates his position, since he wants change, just with familiar elements kept. Option D is the opposite of what Roland actually says he hopes for.
  31. Listening Script

    Nina: Have you seen the plan to turn the empty lot on Baker Street into a community garden?

    Elias: I have, and I love the idea, though I wonder how the plots will actually be assigned to residents.

    Elias: That could be tricky for residents who aren't comfortable using computers or don't have internet access.

    Nina: That's a good point, maybe they should also allow sign ups in person at the community centre.

    Elias: I'll email the organizers and suggest that as an alternative option.

    The correct answer is A (He worries residents without internet access or computer comfort would be excluded): Elias says the online form 'could be tricky for residents who aren't comfortable using computers or don't have internet access.' Option D is incorrect since Elias says he loves the idea of the garden itself. Option B, the lot's size, is never mentioned in the conversation. Option C, how long the process takes, is not what Elias raises as his concern.
  32. Listening Script

    Tomas: The office is debating whether to keep the free coffee machine or switch to a paid one to cut costs.

    Ayesha: I understand wanting to save money, but free coffee is honestly a small perk that keeps morale up.

    Tomas: True, but management says the machine costs more to maintain than people realize.

    Ayesha: Maybe they could keep it free but switch to a cheaper coffee brand to lower the cost.

    Tomas: That seems reasonable. I doubt most people would notice much difference in taste.

    Ayesha: I'll suggest that compromise at the next staff meeting instead of removing it entirely.

    The correct answer is D (Keeping the coffee free but switching to a cheaper coffee brand): Ayesha suggests they 'could keep it free but switch to a cheaper coffee brand to lower the cost.' Option B is the opposite of her suggestion, since she wants to avoid removing the perk entirely. Option C, charging a fee, is the paid option management is already considering, not Ayesha's compromise. Option A is never mentioned anywhere in the conversation.
  33. Listening Script

    A neighbourhood newsletter asked residents about the proposed noise bylaw limiting construction to weekdays only. Here is what three residents said.

    Otis: I work night shifts and sleep during the day, so weekday construction noise still disturbs my rest either way.

    Bianca: I think it's a great idea. My weekends are the only quiet time I get with my kids at home.

    Feliks: I run a small renovation business, and losing weekend work hours would really hurt my income.

    The correct answer is A (Feliks, because losing weekend work hours would hurt his income): Feliks explicitly says 'losing weekend work hours would really hurt my income' as a renovation business owner. Otis, in option C, raises a personal sleep issue unrelated to the weekday-only rule, since noise bothers him either way. Bianca, in option D, expresses appreciation for quiet weekends but does not mention money. Option B is false because Feliks clearly raises a financial concern.
  34. Listening Script

    A local radio show asked listeners for their opinion on banning gas powered leaf blowers within city limits. Here are three responses.

    Rosalind: I support it completely. The noise from those machines every fall is honestly unbearable on my street.

    Deshawn: I run a landscaping company, and switching all my equipment to electric would be a huge upfront cost.

    Priyanka: I don't have strong feelings either way, but I do like that electric ones are quieter for my toddler's naps.

    The correct answer is B (Deshawn, because switching his landscaping equipment would be a huge upfront cost): Deshawn states directly that 'switching all my equipment to electric would be a huge upfront cost.' Rosalind, in option A, supports the ban because of noise, without mentioning cost. Priyanka, in option C, mentions a personal benefit for her toddler's naps, not a cost concern. Option D is false since Rosalind clearly supports the ban rather than opposing it.
  35. Listening Script

    A community survey asked parents what they think about extending the school day by thirty minutes to add an art period. Here is what three parents said.

    Zara: I'm torn, but I think creative subjects matter just as much as math and science for kids' development.

    The correct answer is A (A longer day would mean his son gets home later, affecting homework and dinner): Connor says 'a longer day means my son gets home later, cutting into his homework and dinner time.' Option D misreads Connor, who calls the idea 'nice' rather than dismissing art itself. Option B, classroom space, is never mentioned by any speaker. Option C is actually Zara's viewpoint, not Connor's.
  36. Listening Script

    A podcast asked three coworkers what they think about the company removing assigned desks in favour of open seating. Here is what they said.

    Isla: I hate it. I lost twenty minutes this morning just looking for an open seat near an outlet.

    Marcus: I don't mind either way, but I do miss having a place to keep a photo of my family on my desk.

    The correct answer is D (Isla, because she spent twenty minutes looking for a seat near an outlet): Isla says she 'lost twenty minutes this morning just looking for an open seat near an outlet.' Grant, in option C, expresses a positive view about flexibility, not a time complaint. Marcus, in option A, mentions missing a personal touch, not lost time. Option B is false because Isla clearly describes losing time that morning.
  37. Listening Script

    A town hall meeting asked residents for opinions on adding paid parking to the main street shopping district. Here is what three residents said.

    Louis: I think it makes sense. Right now employees park there all day and customers can never find a spot.

    Delphine: I mostly walk to that street, so honestly paid parking wouldn't change much for me personally.

    The correct answer is C (He thinks it would stop employees from parking there all day so customers can find spots): Louis says paid parking 'makes sense' because currently 'employees park there all day and customers can never find a spot.' Option B describes Harriet's worry, the opposite of Louis's view. Option D describes Delphine's situation, not Louis's. Option A also describes Harriet, who owns the shop and worries about losing customers.
  38. Listening Script

    A local news show asked residents for their thoughts on the city adding a splash pad to the downtown park. Here is what three residents said.

    Anika: I think it's a fantastic use of the space. Families really need more free things to do downtown.

    Callum: I'd rather see that money spent repairing the park's existing playground equipment, which is already falling apart.
